=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf') for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j = n - 1: return d
            for di, dj in [(0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)]:
                ci, cj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ci < m and 0 <= cj < n:
                    if self.lessThan(d + grid[ci][cj], distance[ci][cj]):
                        distance[ci][cj] = self.sum(d, grid[ci][cj])
                        if grid[ci][cj] == 1: q.append((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                        else: q.appendleft((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                else: q.appendleft((distance[i][j], i, j))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

```python
class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf') for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j = n - 1: return d
            for di, dj in [(0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)]:
                ci, cj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ci < m and 0 <= cj < n:
                    if self.lessThan(d + grid[ci][cj], distance[ci][cj]):
                        distance[ci][cj] = self.sum(d, grid[ci][cj])
                        if grid[ci][cj] == 1: q.append((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                        else: q.appendleft((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                else: q.appendleft((distance[i][j], i, j))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.

- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;

Original:

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf') for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j = n - 1: return d
            for di, dj in [(0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)]:
                ci, cj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ci < m and 0 <= cj < n:
                    if self.lessThan(d + grid[ci][cj], distance[ci][cj]):
                        distance[ci][cj] = self.sum(d, grid[ci][cj])
                        if grid[ci][cj] == 1: q.append((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                        else: q.appendleft((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                else: q.appendleft((distance[i][j], i, j))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

Need maybe include `from collections import deque` instead of `import collections`. If using `collections.deque`, import collections. If using `deque`, import from collections. Code:
```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        dq = deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while dq:
            d, i, j = dq.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            dq.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            dq.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))
        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

Let's consider if there is a more minimal fix: Keep original structure but fix syntax and undefined methods and else. Could output:
```
class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        import collections
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf') for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in [(0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)]:
                ci, cj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ci < m and 0 <= cj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ci][cj]
                    if nd < distance[ci][cj]:
                        distance[ci][cj] = nd
                        if grid[ci][cj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ci, cj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ci, cj))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```
This is minimal. Should we include stale skip? It is not strictly necessary but good. The instruction says "fix code and output only fixed code", not necessarily minimal. - m * n <= 10^5
- grid[i][j] is 0 or 1.
I don't remember start/end guarantee. But many solutions initialize `dist[0][0] = 0` and don't count start. If start were 1, answer might be 0? Let's think: If you start at an obstacle, do you need to remove it to "move from upper left corner to lower right corner"? You are already at upper left. The obstacle is in the cell you occupy. To move out, you don't need to remove it? Actually if the cell is an obstacle, can you stand there? The problem says "obstacle that can be removed" maybe all cells are traversable but obstacle removal cost. Starting at obstacle would require removal to be on it? Ambiguous. LeetCode likely guarantees start/end 0. So okay.

Let's write:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

Potential issue with if `grid[ni][nj]` is 1 and `nd` less, but `dist[ni

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
